1.      Please provide an executive summary describing the nature and scope of the proposal. Additionally,
please include the following: (1) identification of all parties, (2) description of the applicant and it's
licensure track record, (3) the type of transaction proposed including description of the transaction and
relevant costs,(4) summary of all transfer documents,(5) summary of the organizational structure of the
applicant and its affiliates, and (6) whether the facility will be accredited.

 Hope Nursing Home Care, LLC (the "Applicant") is requesting Change in Effective Control
("CEC") approval of the planned acquisition of certain assets and the business of Home Care
 Services of Rhode Island, Inc., a Rhode Island corporation ("HCS" or "Facility") held by Vincent
 C. Ward ("Seller"). HCS is a licensed Home Nursing Care Provider with a main office located at
 68 Cumberland Street, Suite 206, Woonsocket, Rhode Island 02895. Since the planned acquisition
 is of the assets and business of HCS (see Q14 response), the HCS assets and business will be added
 to and operated under Applicant's existing Rhode Island home nursing care provider license. As is
 explained in further detail below, the Applicant is part of the Community Based Care family of
 home health care providers.
Community Based Care has a main office located in Morrisville, North Carolina and operates home
based care providers in North Carolina, Virginia, Florida and Rhode Island.                      See:
https://cbcare.com/. The Community Based            Care   operations  are  managed   by  a seasoned
management team let by Michael Kotzen as President and CEO. Mr. Kotzen is responsible for
developing and implementing the overall mission of Community Based Care and ensuring the
delivery of the highest quality service to its clients and families. Mr. Kotzen has more than twenty-
six years of diverse healthcare leadership experience. He earned a Bachelor's degree in Business
Administration from Rutgers University and a MBA from St. Joseph's University. Mr. Kotzen is a
fellow in the American College of Healthcare Executives.
Following the approval of this CEC Application, pursuant to the terms of an Asset Purchase
 Agreement dated September 22, 2022, the Applicant plans to acquire certain of the assets and the
 business of HCS for an aggregate purchase price of $540,000 subject to adjustment at closing
(the "Acquisition"). The Acquisition will be funded 100% through equity. Following the
 Acquisition, the assets and the business of HCS,including the current HCS location in Woonsocket,
 will added to the operations of the Applicant.
 One hundred percent(100%)of the membership interests of the Applicant are held by CBC Rhode
 Island FT, LLC, which in turn is 100% owned by Community Based Care FT Acquisition, LLC
 which is 100% owned by Community Based Care Intermediate I, LP. Community Based Care
 Intermediate I, LP is 99% owned by Community Based Care Holdings, LP and 1% owned by
 Martis Partners III Community Aggregator, LP. Community Based Care Holdings, LP is owned
 14.89% collectively by individuals, 12.48% collectively by co-investors and 72.63% by Martis
 Partners III Community Aggregator, LP. Martis Partners III Community Aggregator, L.P. is
 18.58% owned by Martis Partners III Community Blocker, LP, 1.51% owned by Martis Partners
 III Community Blocker, LLC,and 79.91% owned by Martis Partners III, LP. Martis Partners III
 Community Aggregator, LP is managed by Martis Partners III GP,LLC.
 Martis Partners III Community Blocker, LP and Martis Partners III Commnity Blocker, LLC are
 each 100% owned by Martis Partners III PV, LP. Martis Partners III, LP and Martis Partners III
 PV, LP are owned by passive investors that commit capital to Martis but do not have involvement
 in or control of investment decisions made by Martis or the day to day operations of Applicant.
 Community Based Care Intermediate I, LP also owns 100% of the stock of Community Based Care
 Acquisition, Inc., which owns 100% of the membership interest of Community Based Care, LLC,
 Community Based Care, LLC in turn owns interests in several home health providers, and is
 currently operating in 4 states. See Community Based Care Structure in response to Question
 15. See Tab 1, attached to demonstrate that the passive investors do not have involvement in or
 control aliTistment decisions made by Martis or the day-to-day operations of the Applicant.
 It is planned that, pending approval, the Applicant will continue to provide services from its
 existing location in Cranston and the newly added HCS location in Woonsocket and the expanded

1 1.    Please provide a concise description of the services currently offered by the licensed entity and
identify any services that will be added, terminated, expanded, or reduced and state the reasons therefore:
HCS provides home nursing care services to Rhode Island pediatric and adult patients who require
skilled nursing services and long-term care and supportive services to those in need and their families
and caregivers. HCS services also include assistance with activities of daily living, personal care,
homemaking and companion care.
The Applicant intends to continue with the mission established by HCS to provide patients home
nursing care services to support patients and their families. The Applicant does not anticipate that
any services will be added, terminated, expanded or reduced from those currently offered and
intends to provide services within the scope of its license.

12.     Please identify the long-tetra plans of the applicant with respect to the health care programs and
health care services to be provided at the facility:
The Applicant does not currently anticipate altering the services provided and plans to continue to
provide quality, patient centric home health nursing services to its patients. The Applicant plans to
assume and maintain the current office location of HCS in Woonsocket.

13.     Does the entity seeking licensure plan to participate in Medicare or Medicaid (Titles XVIII or XIX
of the Social Security Act)?
         MEDICARE: Yes           No X                   MEDICAID: Yes X No
        • If response to Question 13 for either Medicare and/or Medicaid is 'No', please explain.
              The current licensee, HCS, does not provide Medicare services. The Applicant does not
              currently service traditional Medicare patients but does contract with a Medicare
              Advantage plan to provide personal care services.

16.    If the proposed owner, operator or director owned, operated or directed a health care facility (both
within and outside Rhode Island) within the past three years, please demonstrate the record of that
person(s) with respect to access of traditionally underserved populations to its health care facilities.

As noted in response to Question 1 and as set forth in Tab 15 Organization Charts, the Applicant
provides services in several states, including Rhode Island and services to traditionally underserved
populations, including Medicaid and charity care services under applicable regulations and policies.
The Applicant believes that it has a strong track record with respect to providing access to
traditionally underserved populations in its service areas.
17.     Please identify the proposed immediate and long-term plans of the applicant to ensure adequate and
appropriate access to the program and health care services to be provided by the health care facility/ies to
traditionally underserved populations.

The Applicant plans to accept all patients regardless of their insurance coverage or ability to
pay. The Applicant will also comply with all applicable policies and procedures required for
serving Medicaid patients.
18.     Please provide a copy of charity care policies and procedures and charity care application form.

The Applicant will provide charity care pursuant to its Charity Care Policy. See Tab 18, attached.

              1.     Shawn L. Taylor et al. v. Community Innovations, Inc. (d/b/a Trotter's Bluff
                     Residential), State of North Carolina, Wake County, General Court of Justice,
                     Superior Court Division, Court File No. 20 CVS 8016).

              This is a personal injury case against Community Innovations, a Community Based
              Care subsidiary. Community Innovations filed a motion to dismiss which was denied.
              The matter is now proceeding to discovery.

              Date of filing: 2020
              Status: Pending

              2.     EEOC v. Community Innovations, Inc., U.S. District Court for the
                     Eastern District of North Carolina (Western Division), Civil Action
                     No. 5:17-CV-00599.

              This was a charge of retaliation under Title VII of the Civil Rights Act. Community
              Innovations (a Community Based Care subsidiary) denied liability but settled the
              matter to avoid further litigation expense.

              Date of filing: December 5, 2017
              Status: Settled - March 20,2019

              3.     Aym Technologies, LLC v. Gene Rodgers, Scopia Capital Management LLP and
                     Community Based Care, LLC, No. 16 CVS 21788(N.C. Business Court)

              Aym was a business dispute between Aym Technologies and the previous owner of
              Community Based Care. Community Based Care was named as a defendant but was
              dismissed. Summary judgment on behalf of all defendants was granted.

              Date of filing: December 5,2017
              Status: Settled - August 2021; dismissal filed Sep. 22,2021

              4.     Scopia Capital Management LP, Homecare Management Corporation and
                     Community Based Care LLC v. Lewis Quinn and Aym Technologies, LLC,Index
                     No. 152069/2017 (Supreme Court of the State of New York, New York
                     County)

              Scopia v. Lewis Quinn was a companion case to Aym filed in New York court. The
              case was settled and has been fully resolved.

              Date of filing: 2017(date unknown)
              Status: Settled - August 2021

              5.    Michael Sunderman v. Deborah Louise Richards and Lindley Habilitation
                    Services, Inc.(Florida)

21.    Please identify any planned actions of the applicant to reduce, limit, or contain health care costs
and improve the efficiency with which health care services are delivered to the citizens of this state.

Using its patient centric local approach to care that is supported by the resources and experience
of the larger Community Based Care organization, the Applicant plans to improve efficiency of
care in a cost effective manner. The Applicant believes that it has the experience to effectively
manage costs without adversely affecting care delivery and is well positioned to respond to
rapidly evolving health care reform efforts.
22.    Please provide a copy of the Quality Assurance Policies (for the services) and a detailed
explanation of how quality assurance for patient services will be implemented at the facility/ies by the
applicant.

See Tab 22, attached for copies of the Licensee's Quality Assurance related policies.                  The
Applicant intends to continue operating the Licensee in accordance with such policies.

23.   Please provide a detailed description about the amount and source of the equity and debt
commitment for this transaction.(NOTE: If debt is contemplated as part of the financing, please complete
Appendix E). Additionally, please demonstrate the following:
       A. The immediate and long-term financial feasibility ofthe proposed financing plan;

              HCS has operated as a profitable, stable home and health care provider for many years
              and the Applicant expects that the addition of the HCS business to the Applicant's
              existing Rhode Island operations will allow for continuing positive operational results
              following the Acquisition. As set forth in Appendix A, the Applicant will have adequate
              resources to meet capital and operating needs following the Acquisition. Further, the
              Applicant will continue to be operated as a Community Based Care entity and
              Community Based Care successfully operates home care and related healthcare provider
              entities in other states and has the financial resources and capabilities to support the
              Applicant's financial needs in the event of any operational deficiencies.
        B. The relative availability offunds for capital and operating needs; and

              As noted in Appendix A, the Applicant is projected to have sufficient funds for capital
              and operating needs.
        C. The applicant's financial capability.

              The financial statements of Community Based Care in Tab 28, attached, demonstrate the
              Applicant's financial capability.

24.    Please provide legally binding evidence of site control (e.g., deed, lease, option, etc.) sufficient to
enable the applicant to have use and possession of the subject property, if applicable.

See Tab 24, attached. The Applicant intends to maintain the current HCS location in
Woonsocket and to add it to its existing operations based in the Applicant's Cranston office
location.

 Certificate (this "Certificate"), dated as of the date hereof.

       capacity as a Manager Member, is duly authorized to act on behalfof the General
       Partner.

                                     ASSET PURCHASE AGREEMENT

        This Asset Purchase Agreement (this "Agreement"), dated as of                      24
                                                                                            2022
(the "Effective Date"), is entered into by and among Home Care   Services of Rhode Island, Inc., a
 Rhode Island corporation ("Seller"), Vincent q.'WEird, the owner of Seller("Owner"), and   Hope
 Nursing Home Care Care, LLC, a Rhode Islkiinimited liability company ("Buyer") (each, a
"Party" and, collectively, the "Parties").

                                                  RECITALS

           WHEREAS,Seller is a provider of home care services (the "Business"); and

        WHEREAS, Seller wishes to sell and assign certain assets to Buyer, and Buyer wishes to
 purchase and assume from Seller, the rights and obligations of Seller to the Purchased Assets and
 the Assumed Liabilities (as defined herein), subject to the terms and conditions set forth herein.

         NOW,THEREFORE,in consideration of the mutual covenants and agreements hereinafter
 set forth and for other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which are
 hereby acknowledged, the Parties hereto agree as follows:

                                                ARTICLE I
                                             PURCHASE AND SALE

         Section 1.01 Purchase and Sale of Assets. Subject to the terms and conditions set forth

        Section 1.04 Purchase Price; Payment at Closing.
       (a)      The aggregate purchase price (the "Purchase Price") to be paid by Buyer to Seller
for the Purchased  Assets shall be Five Hundred Forty Thousand Dollars($540,000). The Purchase
Price shall be subject to adjustment by Buyer based on the financial performance of Seller in the
three-month period prior to the Closing, which shall be calculated as the difference between the
annualized EBITDA from the three-month period prior to the Closing and $135,000, multiplied
by 4.0x resulting in a potential decrease, increase, or no change to the Purchase Price. Seller shall
provide to Buyer updated financial statements on a monthly basis and shall provide the final
financial statements for the three-month period prior to Closing at least four(4)business days prior
to Closing.
       (b)      At the Closing., Buyer shall pay the Closing Cash Payment (as defined below) to
Seller by wire transfer of immediately available funds pursuant to Seller's written wire instructions
provided to Buyer at least two (2) Business Days prior to the Closing Date. The "Closing Cash
Payment" shall be calculated as follows:

        Section 1.07 Accounts Receivable; Certain Receipts by Buyer and Seller.
       (a)      Seller shall retain whatever right, title, and interest it may have in and to all

period prior to and on the Closing Date ("Seller Receivables"). Seller shall have full authority to
collect Seller Receivables, and Buyer shall have no obligation to collect any Seller Receivable on

 I 0345261 6.Docx
                                                   4

===== PAGE 84 =====
forwarded by Buyer to Seller, along with the applicable remittance advice, within ten (10) days
after receipt thereof, if the applicable remittance advice identifies that such payment relates to the
period on or prior to the Closing Date.
       (c)     Payments received by Seller for accounts receivable which relate to services
provided by the Business during the period following the Closing Date ("Buyer Receivables")
from third-party payors, including, but not limited to, Medicare, Medicaid, managed care, and
health insurance, shall be forwarded by Seller to Buyer within ten (10) days following the end of
the calendar month in which they are received if such payments specifically indicate on the
accompanying remittance advice that such payments relate to the period after the Closing Date.
       (d)     If any payment discharges both Seller Receivables and Buyer Receivables (a
"Mixed Payment"), then the Party receiving the Mixed Payment shall forward the amount
constituting the other Party's receivables to the other Party within ten (10) days following the end
of the calendar month in which they are received.
       (c)     If the Parties cannot reasonably deten-nine which portion of a Mixed Payment
constitutes Seller Receivables and which portion constitutes Buyer Receivables, then the
unidentifiable portion of the Mixed Payment shall be pro-rated between the Parties based on the
total number of days of service set forth on a remittance advice or applicable supporting records
and documentation, with Seller receiving the pro-rated portion attributable to the period prior to
and including the Closing Date, and Buyer receiving the pro-rated portion attributable to the period
after the Closing Date. Any such pro-rated amount of a Mixed Payment shall be paid to the non-
receiving Party within ten (10) days following the end of the calendar month in which they are
received.

        (f)     The Parties agree to confer as needed for up to six(6) months following the Closing
 to reconcile payments to ensure that payment is remitted to the proper Party.
        (g)     On the Closing Date, Seller shall provide Buyer with an updated list of all known
 Seller Receivables. Ifother receivables relating to the time period prior to the Closing Date become
 known after the Closing Date, Seller shall retain the right to receive payment from Buyer in
 accordance with the above terms in this Section 1.07.

 and scope and under its circumstances.

 corporation duly incorporated, validly existing, and in good standing under the laws of the State

thereunder and to consummate the transactions contemplated hereby and thereby. The execution,

 which it is a party, and the consummation of the transactions contemplated hereby and thereby,
have been duly authorized by all requisite corporate action on the part of Seller. This Agreement
has been (and, as of the Closing, the other Transaction Documents to which Seller is a party will
have been) duly executed and delivered by Seller, and (assuming due authorization, execution, and
delivery by Buyer of this Agreement and the other Transaction Documents to which Buyer is a
party) this Agreement constitutes (and, as of the Closing, the other Transaction Documents to
which Seller is a party will constitute) legal, valid, and binding obligations of Seller, enforceable
against Seller in accordance with their respective terms, except as such enforceability may be
limited by bankruptcy, insolvency, reorganization, moratorium or similar laws affecting creditors'
rights generally and by general principles of equity (regardless of whether enforcement is sought
in a proceeding at law or in equity).

without notice or lapse of time or both) any violation of, or default under, or give rise to a right of

contract or other instrument to which Seller is a party or to which any of the Purchased Assets are
subject; or (d) result in the creation or imposition of any Encumbrance on the Purchased Assets.

                                           ARTICLE VII
                                          INDEMNIF1CATION

         Section 7.01 Survival. Subject to the limitations and other provisions of this Agreement,
the representations and warranties contained herein shall survive the Closing and shall remain in
full force and effect until the date that is one (1) year from the Closing Date; provided, that the
representations and warranties in Section 3.01, Section 3.02, Section 3.04, Section 3.05, Section
3.14, Section 3.19, Section 4,01, Section 4.02, Section 4.03, Section 5.01, Section 5.02, Section
5.04 and Section 5,05 shall survive indefinitely, and the representations and warranties in Section
3.18 shall survive for the full period of all applicable statutes of limitations (giving effect to any
waiver, mitigation or extension thereof) plus 60 days. All covenants and agreements ofthe Parties
contained herein shall survive the Closing indefinitely or for the period explicitly specified therein,
Notwithstanding the foregoing, any claims asserted in good faith with reasonable specificity (to
the extent known at such time) and in writing by notice from the non-breaching Party to the
breaching Party prior to the expiration date of the applicable survival period shall not thereafter be
barred by the expiration of the relevant representation or warranty and such claims shall survive
until finally resolved.

        Section 7.02 Indemnification by Seller and Owner. Subject to the other terms and
conditions of this Article VII, Seller and Owner shall jointly and severally defend, indemnify, and
hold harmless Buyer, its Affiliates and their respective members, managers, directors, officers, and
employees (collectively, the "Buyer Indemnitees") from and against all LosseS, arising from or
relating to:
     (a)     any inaccuracy in or breach of any of the representations or warranties of Seller or
Owner contained in this Agreement or the other Transaction Documents;
      (b)     any breach or non-fulfillment of any covenant, agreement, or obligation to be
performed by Seller or Owner pursuant to this Agreement or the other Transaction Documents; or
       (c)         any Excluded Asset or Excluded Liability,

 10345261 6.Docx
                                                  24

===== PAGE 104 =====
        Section 7.03 Indemnification by Buyer. Subject to the other terms and conditions of
this Article VII, Buyer shall defend, indemnify, and hold harmless Seller and Owner, their
respective Affiliates and their respective stockholders, members, directors. officers, and
employees (collectively, the "Seller Indemnitees") from and against all Losses arising from or
relating to:
      (a)      any inaccuracy in or breach of any of the representations or warranties of Buyer
contained in this Agreement or the other Transaction Documents;
      (b)     any breach or non-fulfillment of any covenant, agreement or obligation to be
performed by Buyer pursuant to this Agreement or the other Transaction Documents; or
         (c)          any Assumed Liability.

        Section 7.04 Indemnification Procedures. Whenever any claim shall arise for
indemnification under Section 7.02 or Section 7.03, as applicable, the Person entitled to
indemnification (the "Indemnified Party")shall promptly provide written notice of such claim to
the Party(ies) obligated to provide such indemnification (the "Indemnifying Party"). The failure
to give such prompt written notice shall not, however, relieve the Indemnifying Party of its
indemnification obligations, except and only to the extent that the Indemnifying Party forfeits
rights or defenses by reason of such failure. Such notice by the Indemnified Party shall describe
the claim in reasonable detail, shall include copies of all material written evidence thereof and
shall indicate the estimated amount, if reasonably practicable, of the Loss that has been or may be
sustained by the Indemnified Party. In connection with any Third Party Claim giving rise to
indemnity under Section 7.02 or Section 7.03,respectively, the Indemnifying Party, at its sole cost
and expense and upon written notice to the Indemnified Party, may assume the defense of any such
Third Party Claim with counsel reasonably satisfactory to the Indemnified Party. The Indemnified
Party shall be entitled to participate in (but not control) the defense of any such Third Party Claim,
with its counsel and at its own cost and expense. If the Indemnifying Party does not assume the
defense of any such Third Party Claim, the Indemnified Party may, but shall not be obligated to,
defend against such Third Party Claim in such manner as it may deem appropriate, including, but
not limited to, settling such Third Party Claim, after giving notice of it to the Indemnifying Party,
on such terms as the Indemnified Party may deem appropriate and no action taken by the
Indemnified Party in accordance with such defense and settlement shall relieve the Indemnifying
Party of its indemnification obligations herein provided with respect to any damages resulting
therefrom. The Indemnifying Party shall not settle any Third Party Claim without the Indemnified
Party's prior written consent(which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld or delayed).

       Section 7.05 Tax Treatment of Indemnification Payments. All indemnification
payments made by Seller or Owner under this Agreement shall be treated by the Parties as an
adjustment to the Purchase Price for tax purposes, unless otherwise required by law.

       Section 7.06 Effect of Investigation. Buyer's right to indemnification or other remedy
based on the representations and warranties of Seller and Owner contained herein will not be

11 1034526 I_6.Docx
                                                 25

===== PAGE 105 =====
 affected by any investigation conducted by Buyer with respect to, or any knowledge acquired by
 Buyer at any time, with respect to the accuracy or inaccuracy of any such representation or
 warranty.

        Section 7.07 Indemnification Limitations and Conditions. The indemnification
 provided for in Section 7.02 and Section 7.03 above is subject to the following limitations and
 conditions:
       (a)      Seller and Owner shall not be liable to the Buyer Indemnitees under Section
7.02(a), and Buyer shall not be liable to the Seller Indemnitees under Section 7.03(a), until the
aggregate amount of all Losses in respect of indemnification under Section 7.02(a) or Section
7.03(a), respectively, exceeds $10,000 (the "Basket"), in which event Seller and Owner or Buyer,
as the case may be, shall be liable only for any Losses in excess of the Basket.
       (b)     The aggregate amount of all Losses for which Seller and Owner or Buyer shall be
liable pursuant to Sections Section 7.02(a) and Section 7.03(a), respectively, shall not exceed
$60,000 (the "Cap").
      (c)      The aggregate amount of all Losses for which Seller and Owner shall be liable
pursuant to Section 7.02 shall not exceed the Purchase Price.
       (d)      Any Losses payable to a Buyer Indemnitee pursuant to this Article VII shall be
satisfied from Seller and Owner,jointly and severally.
       (e)     Notwithstanding anything contained herein to the contrary, all "material,"
"Material Adverse Effect" and similar materiality type qualifications contained in the
representations and warranties shall be ignored for purposes of determining the amount of any
Losses incurred with respect to the indemnification provisions hereof,

        Section 7.09 Exclusive Remedies. Subject to Section 10.13, the indemnification
provisions of this Article VII shall be the exclusive remedy for all monetary damages arising from
matters covered by the provisions of Sections 7.02 and 7.03 (regardless of the legal theory
advanced in support ofsuch claims) except in the case offraud, in which case a Party shall be able
to seek any statutory or common law remedy it may have.

                      MEDICAL RECORD CUSTODIAN AGREEMENT

        This'Medical Record Custodian Agreement (this "MR Agreement") is made and entered
 into as of               , 2022 (the 'Effective Date') by and between HOME CARE
 SERVICES OF RHODE ISLAND, INC., a Rhode Island corporation ("Seller's), and HOPE
 NURSING HOME CARE,LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company ("Buyer").
                                          P. 0
       WHEREAS, Seller, Vincent            Ward, and Buyer entered into an Asset Purchase
Agreement(the "Agreement"), dated as of 3V( 22.             , 2022, pursuant to which Seller and
Owner agreed to sell, and Buyer agreed to purchase, certain assets of Seller's business that offers
home care services to help individuals living in Rhode Island (the Susiness'), pursuant to terms
and conditions set forth therein:

        WHEREAS, Seller wishes to ensure the continuation of necessary health care set-vices for
clients of the Business and to provide for the necessary access of Buyer to the Client Records (as
defined below) so that the Client Records may be accessed readily by health care providers that
will provide continuing treatment to the clients following the consummation of the transactions
contemplated by the Agreement; and

       WHEREAS, the parties' execution and delivery of this MR Agreement is a condition
precedent to the consummation of the transactions contemplated by the Agreement.

       NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the foregoing recitals, the mutual promises
contained herein, as well as other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of
which are hereby acknowledged, the parties hereto agree as follows:

    I. Access to and Custody of Client Records.

       (a)      From the Effective Date through the end of the Retention Period (as defined
below), Seller shall provide Buyer with access to those certain active medical records of the
clients of the Business who are listed in de-identified form on Exhibit 1 attached hereto (the
`Clients'), including paper records and electronic records (together, the 'Client Records'),
subject to (i) the rights of the Clients to authorize the transfer of the Client Records, (ii) the
Clients' right of access to the information contained in their records, if applicable, and (iii) all
privacy and confidentiality requirements imposed by state or federal law or regulation.

       (b)     The parties hereto agree that access to the active Client Records is being granted
to Buyer hereunder for purposes of the care and treatment of Clients by Buyer and its employees
and staff from and after the Effective Date, and the billing activities of Buyer related to such care
and the operations of the Business as operated by Buyer.

     (c)     If Buyer makes its own copy of the Client Records, it shall store and maintain that
copy in accordance with applicable federal, state, and local laws, rules, and regulations,

===== PAGE 119 =====
 including those laws, rules, and regulations governing the confidentiality and security of patient
 protected health information, and Buyer shall take all reasonable measures to protect the copy of
 the Client Records from theft, loss, unauthorized destruction, and unauthorized access.

      (d)     Seller shall maintain custody of the Client Records, complete through the date
immediately prior to the Effective Date, for the minimum retention period applicable by law (the
"Retention Period").

    2. Client Communication and Access.

       (a)      Seller shall provide timely access to, and photocopies of, the Client Records to
Clients and their legal representatives to the extent, and during the timeframes, required by laws,
rules, or regulations applicable to the parties hereto, and to other individuals, entities, and
governmental agencies that have the right to access and/or receive photocopies of Client
Records. Such access and copies shall be provided within the time periods required by applicable
laws, rules, and regulations. Seller may charge appropriate copying fees, consistent with its then-
existing policies and procedures and the requirements of applicable laws, rules, and regulations.
The form of authorizationlrelease for Client Records which shall be utilized for such purpose
shall be compliant with applicable federal and state law.

       (b)       Seller acknowledges that Seller remains the primary custodian of all medical
records and that Buyer is only being provided with access to the Client Records of Seller. Seller
is solely responsible for responding to any audit inquiries or other inquiries for Client Records
that pertain to periods prior to the Effective Date of this MR Agreement.

   3. Legal and 1-11PAA Compliance. Buyer acknowledges that the Client Records it is being
given access to hereunder are confidential. Buyer shall comply with all provisions of applicable
law, rules and regulations with respect to the confidentiality and security ofsuch Client Records.

    4. Term and Termination.

      (a)       This MR Agreement shall take effect as of the Effective Date and shall expire at
the end of the Retention Period, unless earlier terminated in accordance herewith.

      (b)     This MR Agreement may be terminated at any time by the mutual written
agreement of the parties hereto.

   5. Miscellaneous.

      (a)      Neither party hereto may assign this MR Agreement without the express prior
written consent of the other party hereto, and any attempt to assign this MR Agreement without
such consent shall be void.

===== PAGE 120 =====
       (b)      Neither party hereto shall be authorized to act as agent for the other or to incur
any liability in the name of or on behalf of the other, unless specifically authorized in this MR
Agreement or in a writing executed by the party hereto that would be responsible for the
obligation.

      (c)      This MR Agreement shall be governed by the laws of the State of North Carolina,
exclusive of conflict of law rules.

      (d)       Nothing express or implied in this MR Agreement. is intended to confer, nor shall
anything herein confer, upon any person other than a party hereto any rights, remedies,
obligations, or liabilities whatsoever.

       (e)      This MR Agreement is the entire agreement between the parties hereto
concerning the subject matter hereof and supersedes all prior agreements, whether written or
oral, concerning the subject matter hereof. This MR Agreement shall not be changed or
modified, except by a writing signed by both of the parties hereto and their respective successors.
Except as otherwise provided herein, all rights and remedies set forth in this MR Agreement
shall be in addition to and not exclusive of any other rights or remedies now or hereafter existing
at law or equity.

       (f)      Any notice, request, instruction, or document to be given hereunder by any party
hereto to another shall be in writing and delivered personally or mailed, first class registered or
certified mail, postage prepaid, or by reputable overnight courier, to the address of such other
party hereto stated below:

the Client Records.

       (k)     This MR Agreement shall he binding upon and shall inure to the benefit of the

===== PAGE 127 =====
                              CBC Charity Care Policy


PURPOSE
To identify the criteria to be applied when accepting patients for charity care

POLICY
Patients without third-party payer coverage and who are unable to pay for medically
necessary care will be accepted for charity care admission, per established criteria.
Community Based Care will establish objective criteria and financial screening
procedures for determining eligibility for charity care.
The organization will consistently apply the Financial Assistance policy.

 PROCEDURE
When it is identified that the patient has no source for payment of services and requires
medically necessary care/service, the patient must provide personal financial
information upon which the determination of charity care will be made.

An administrator will meet with the patient to determine potential eligibility for financial
assistance from other community resources.

The administrator, along with the appropriate Program Director, will review all
applicable patient information, including financial declarations, physician (or other
authorized licensed independent practitioner) orders, initial assessment information,
and notes to determine acceptance for charity care.

An initial application will be completed by the patient and/or their legal representative
with information which will be used to determine the patient's acceptance for charity
care.

The application will be submitted to the Chief Financial Officer/Business office
designee for approval.

All documentation utilized in the determination for acceptance for charity care will be
maintained in the patient's billing record.

When financial declarations reveal the patient is able to make partial payment for
services, the Administrator, with the appropriate program director, will determine the
sliding-fee schedule to be implemented.

The revised sliding-fee schedule will be presented to the patient for agreement and
signature.

===== PAGE 128 =====
After acceptance for charity care, the patient's ability to pay will be reassessed every
60-90 days.

When the organization is unable to admit the patient or to continue charity care, every
effort will be made to refer the patient for appropriate care/service with an alternate
provider.

The referral source will be advised of acceptance, non-acceptance, continuation, or
discharge from charity care.

A patient/legal representative wishing to appeal the financial assistance determination
can submit an appeal in writing to the Chief Financial Officer by email at:
kduarte cr cbcare.com or by fax to (919) 390-1888. The CFO will review information
and provide final determination within five (5) business days of receipt of the appeal
letter.

===== PAGE 129 =====
                              HOPE NURSING HOME CARE




                                   Quality Assurance Plan

                                    CONFIDENTIALITY

Documentation and communication used in Quality Assurance activities is confidential. All data
collection, measurement, and reporting improvement activities will not contain any identifiable
patient or staff information, in accordance with HIPAA regulation. All reports from Quality
Assurance activities remain the property ofthe agency. Information whether patient or proprietary,
is released only with proper authorization.

===== PAGE 130 =====
I. Quality Assurance Plan Objectives

The Quality Assurance Plan is designed to ensure high quality care and customer satisfaction in
all areas of practice, care, and service delivery. Performance is continuously measured to increase
patient safety, prevent and reduce adverse events and the incidence of sentinel events.

The Quality Assurance process shall be a team based process that systematically measures,
assesses and improves the organization's performance to improve patient and organizational
outcomes. The performance measurement process will provide a structure that will ensure all
employees are aligned and focused on the performance needs ofthe organization and on improving
performance capabilities.

    1. Reflect the Mission in all organizational activities.

   2. Improve efficiency and effectiveness of patient care and services by collecting data to
      monitor its performance.

    3. Meet and exceed patient and referral source expectations.

    4. Continually improve staff and patient knowledge and understanding regarding the use of
       equipment, supplies, and care.

    5. Improve and enhance the standards of service and care utilizing a systematic performance
       improvement approach.

    6. Identify areas for improvement in the operational, clinical, and financial policies and
       procedures and implement a plan for improvement that incorporates principles of
       continuous quality improvement(CQI).
    7. An ongoing, proactive program for identifying and reducing unanticipated adverse events
       and safety risks to patients is defined and implemented.

II. Leadership

Leadership has active involvement and participation in the PI plan. The leadership is
knowledgeable in Quality Assurance processing and methodologies.

This is evidenced by education, reading, and involvement in the development of the written plan
for Quality Assurance and activities, attendance and active participation at the Performance
Improvement meetings.

The leaders set performance improvement activities and identify how the organization adjusts
priorities in response to unusual or urgent events.

Patient services are provided through a collaborative effort of all members of the agency. The
leadership ensures that all staff are dedicated and trained in the processing and methodologies of

                                                 2

===== PAGE 131 =====
performance improvement.

Priorities for monitoring activities will be established through the Quality Assurance meetings.
Prioritizing of Quality Assurance activities will be based on high risk, high volume and problem
prone activities. Activities will focus on patient outcomes, but will also include structure and
process outcomes.

Resources for an organizational wide Quality Assurance program are allocated through the
assignment of staff and the provision oftime for staffto participate in Quality Assurance activities.
Additional resources are provided through development and use of data collection tools, clearly
defined reporting mechanisms, education in the development ofindicators, and analysis of results.
To enhance the program Leadership will actively participate at the Quality Assurance meetings
and foster communication of activities and results throughout the agency. Leadership is ultimately
responsible for overseeing and directing the organization's Quality Assurance program.

III. Scope of Care and Services

Hope Nursing Home Care provides personal care, homemaking, private duty nursing and
community resource coordination to a population of patients that includes but is not limited to
the adult, pediatric and disabled population.

Services are provided to patients of all ages, regardless of race, color and creed, sex, sexual
orientation, national or ethnic origin or payment source.

The care, services and equipment provided may include but are not limited to:
       Personal Care
       Homemaking
       Private Duty Nursing

IV. Major Functions and Processes

Patient Focused Functions
• Rights & Responsibilities of the Individual
• Provision of Care and Services
• Surveillance, Prevention and Control of Infection
• Record of Care and Services

Organizational Functions:
• Improving Organization Performance
• Leadership
• Environmental Safety
• Emergency Management
• Management of Human Resources
• Information Management
• National Patient Safety Goals

Major functions and processes are monitored to reflect the agency's provision of care and
                                                  3

===== PAGE 132 =====
service. They are reflective ofthose that are most important to the health and safety of patients
served. Each major functional process will be measured to determine the existing level of
performance so that informed judgments can be made about the stability of the existing
processes, opportunities for improvement can be identified, the process can be redesigned, and
the redesigned process can be measured to see it meets the organization's objectives.

A high priority will be placed on monitoring those functions and processes which:
1. Occur frequently or affect large numbers of patients.
2. May place patients at risk for serious consequences or deprivation of substantial benefit
   when:
   a. care/service is not provided correctly
   b. care/service is not provided when indicated
   c. care/service is provided when not indicated
   d. produce problems for patients or staff

 Also reviewed will be: healthcare trends that warrant study, particularly in relation to quality of care
 issues, actual/potential adverse healthcare outcomes that warrant study, improvement in delivery of
 healthcare that would have a positive impact on patient care and/or clinical practice and benefits for
 client care and/or clinical practice that are worthy of expenditures to implement study.

 V. Performance Measures

 Measurement is the foundation for performance improvement. Indicators are objective,
 measurable, based on current knowledge and clinical experience and identify opportunities for
 improvement. Performance measures reflect structures of care (resources), process of care
(procedures, techniques) or outcomes of care (complications, or patient progress). Performance
 measures focus on all major functions and processes of the agency.

 Priorities are set to determine the processes and activities that have the greatest significance in
 fulfilling the Mission, the needs/expectations of patients, staff, and the community. Performance
 activities are re-prioritized as needed in response to unusual or urgent events.

 Performance benchmarking is an on-going activity designed to encourage continuous
 improvement in all processes by setting target goals according to identified "best practices."

 Performance measures for processes that are known to jeopardize the safety of patients or are
 associated with sentinel events will be routinely monitored. At a minimum, performance
 measures related to the following processes are monitored:

    • Management of hazardous conditions
    • National patient satisfaction and safety goals


 Benchmarks or thresholds that trigger intensive assessment and evaluation are established.

 VI. Systematic Performance Improvement Approach


                                                   4

===== PAGE 133 =====
A. Measurement

Data collection is continuously performed to monitor the stability of existing processes, identify
opportunities for improvement, identify changes that lead to improvement or sustain
improvement. Data collection is based on the frequency of the activity monitored; the
significance ofthe activity and the extent to which the activity is monitored is problem free.
Sources of data collection include, but are not limited to:

   • Staff needs and suggestions for improving patient safety
   • Staff willingness to reports unanticipated adverse events
   • Environmental conditions
   • Patient needs, expectations, and satisfaction
   • Perception of Care Surveys
   • Patient letters and/or comments regarding services
   • Risk management
   • Quality control
   • Infection control
   • Utilization Review
   • Record Review
   • FMEA
   • Accident/incident reports
   • Performance improvement activity form
   • Structure, process and outcome study data
   • Staff orientation, in-service and continuing education records
   • Inter/Intradepartmental committee meeting minutes
   • Formal group meetings
   • Informal group meetings
   • Accreditation/licensure/certification survey requirements
   • Client classification data
   • Client care standards
   • Personnel records
   • Financial reports
   • Other data relating directly/indirectly to client care
   • External Data sources, including but not limited to the following:
         ■ Professional organizations
         ▪ Regulatory agencies
         ■ Continuing education conferences
         ■ Professional conferences
         ■ Professional literature




Indicators are developed based on current knowledge and best practice. Reliable performance
thresholds are established for each indicator, against which current and future performance is
                                                 5

===== PAGE 134 =====
evaluated. Thresholds are based on current knowledge, staff experience, past performances,
patient needs and expectations, external benchmarks, and the degree of seriousness of the
identified improvement process.

Data collection is used to determine actions needed to improve processes and whether or not they
were effective. Examples of various actions are: redesigning of a process, implementing a new
process, changes in policies and procedures, changes in communication chains, changes in
organizational structures, changes in documentation and forms, and changes in in-service and
training practices. Where undesirable variation in performance occurs, intensive assessments are
initiated. These may include:

• Sentinel events — Sentinel events are defined as an unexpected occurrence or variation
  involving death or serious physical or psychological injury, or the risk thereof. Serious
  injury specifically includes loss of limb or function. The phrase "or risk thereof' includes
  any process variation for which a re-occurrence would carry a significant chance of a serious
  adverse outcome.
• variance of outcomes from desired levels
• hazardous conditions


The agency strives to proactively identify and reduce risks to the patients and staff it serves; with
the goal of preventing adverse events. In order to ensure patient safety, at least one high-risk
process will be selected annually for analysis. A high-risk process is defined as one that if not
planned or implemented correctly, has significant potential for impacting the safety of the
patient. The analysis will be performed using a "failure mode" methodology and should include:
    • Description of the process, through flow charting
    • Identification of the ways in which the process could breakdown or fail,
    • Identification of the possible effects the failure of the process could have on patient,
    • Prioritizing the possible failures,
    • Determining why the prioritized failures could occur,
    • Redesigning the process or underlying systems to minimize the risk to patients,
    • Implementation and ongoing monitoring of the redesigned process.




 The PDCA approach is utilized as a performance improvement approach to assist the
                                                  6

===== PAGE 135 =====
organizational Performance Improvement activities.
                                                     Pe


                                           PDCA Cycle



                                     N,3               co6
PDCA includes four phases of problem solving to achieve Performance Improvement:

Plan:         Define the problem
              Determine the impact if problem not resolved
              Define desired outcome

Do:            Decide what you need to know
               Collect data
               Assess and Interpret data
               Define most influential factors
               Identify root causes of problem

Check/Study: Develop a solution
             Develop an implementation plan
Act:         Gain commitment from management
             Execute the plan
             Measure and record performance to determine impact


B. Assessment

Data is aggregated and analyzed to draw conclusions about the organization's performance and
to determine what is done, how well services and care are provided and if performance is
acceptable. Aggregation and analysis is performed at a frequency appropriate to the activity or
process being evaluated, using statistical tools and techniques. The results of the data analysis
are compared internally over time and with external sources when available.

The following are questions that, as applicable, should be asked during the assessment phase.

a. What is the current level of performance?
b. Is the level of performance within acceptable standards or predefined performance
   expectations as established by policy and procedure and operational practices, and other
   similar organizations or databases?
c. Is the level of performance consistent with the company mission?
d. Are there patterns or trends developing that indicate improvement in a process may be
   required?
e. Do the performance measures indicate that changes or previously implemented solutions
   resulted in an improvement in performance?
f. Do the Satisfaction Survey responses indicate that patients perceive that their needs were
                                                 7

===== PAGE 136 =====
   met?
g. Is the level of performance the same as, better than or less than the previous month or
   quarter?
h. Is performance acceptable, but there is a desire and ability to continue to make
   improvements?
i. Is there a single event that is significant or important enough to require more intensive
   investigation and/or result in a redesign of a current process or practice?
k. Are there confirmed equipment defects or failures that resulted in injury or death to the
   patient and have been identified as sentinel event?
1. What do outcome performance measures indicate about current care processes, what we do
   and how we provide care and services, in relation to the dimensions of performance?

Indicators are continually assessed with respect to customer service, the current professional
practice standards, and continued relevance. To foster improvement opportunities the
organization encourages the use of internal and external benchmarking.

C. Improvement

The systematic performance improvement approach, PDSA is used to identify an opportunity for
improvement or problems. Once an opportunity or problem is identified, an Action Plan is
developed and implemented. Action Plans should determine what is needed to accomplish the
desired outcome, identify the person(s) responsible, tasks to be accomplished, and the target date
for completion. Input should be obtained from all individuals involved in the process to assure
effective solutions are implemented.

An Action Plan is:
  • written in clear, concrete language; describes the process or outcome the is to be
      improved;
  • relates to best practices;
  • practical and achievable; and
  • specific to the identified problem.

If a significant problem or new improvement priority is identified, a Quality Action Team(QAT)
will be formed to complete a more intensive assessment utilizing a formal performance
improvement approach such as PDSA. The QAT involves all applicable departments and
represents all individuals involved in the process being assessed. Whether the action plan is
developed for a minor problem identified or developed by the Quality Action Team, the goal of
an action plan is to design new processes, improve or change work processes that do not meet the
company or customer's expectations, industry accepted standards of practice in order to improve
patient safety and reduce sentinel events.




D. Communication

Findings, conclusions, recommendations, and results will be disseminated to managers and staff

                                                 8

===== PAGE 137 =====
after each Performance Improvement meeting. Leadership is advised of all findings quarterly.
Leadership will use results ofPerformance Improvement as a measure of the care and services
provided and to identify and define opportunities for improvement in order to meet the goals of
the mission statement.

When an improvement in the way the agency does business, provides care and services has been
accepted as a policy, practice or standard operating procedure, all applicable individuals will
receive communication and an implementation plan, from an appropriately designated
individual(s).

Process Improvements that are developed and or have been proven to be effective, efficient and
improve positive patient outcomes will be implemented company wide.

VII. Quality Action Team

In order to meet the goals of the mission statement, the Quality Action Team will meet as often
as needed. Quality Action Teams will include a cross-section of people who represent some part
of the process or problem being investigated.

The QAT members may be composed of a member from each aspect of our service.
1. Administrator
2. RN case manager
3. Billing/Support Staff
4. Human resource representative
5. Paraprofessionals

Team membership may include as little as 2 people or up to 10 participants. Most group
memberships will range from 2 to 5 individuals.

At times the organization may request participation in Quality Assurance from individuals
outside of the agency. These individuals may be physicians, referral sources or third party
payers. The assistance ofthese outside individuals helps in our quest to exceed customer
expectations for services, care, and safety. Persons not employed by the agency will be required
to sign a confidentiality statement/Business Associate Agreement. All patient information will
be absent of patient names and identifying data in accordance with HIPPA law.

VIII. Evaluation of the Plan

Annually or as often as needed, the Quality Assurance plan will be evaluated for effectiveness
and relationship to current needs. The summary of the findings will be presented to Leadership.

Section 22. Bilis and Notices. Any notice, consent, request, bill, demand or statement hereunder
by either party to the other party shall be in writing and, if received at Landlord's or Tenant's address,
shall be deemed to have been duly given when either delivered or served personally or mailed in a
postpaid envelope, certified mail, return receipt requested, deposited in the United States mails or
sent by nationally recognized overnight courier, addressed as follows:

If to Landlord:

68 Cumberland Street, LLC
P.O Box 569
Barrington, RI 02806

If to Tenant:
Home Care Services of RI, Inc.
68 Cumberland Street, Unit #206
Woonsocket, RI 02895


Either party may at any time change the address for such notices, consents, requests, bills, demands
or statements by delivering or mailing, as aforesaid, to the other party a notice stating the change and
setting forth the changed address, provided such changed address is within the United States. All
bills and statements for reimbursement or other payments or charges due from Tenant to Landlord
hereunder shall be due and payable in full thirty (30) days (unless herein otherwise provided) after
submission thereof by Landlord to Tenant.

Section 23. Successors and Assiens. Subject to Section 12 hereof, the covenants, agreements,
terms, provisions and conditions of Ihis Lease shall bind and benefit the successors and assigns of
the parties hereto with the same effect as if mentioned in each instance where a party hereto is named
or referred to.

===== PAGE 156 =====
Section 24. Amendment Required by Lender. Tenant agrees that upon request by Landlord, it
will execute such amendments to this Lease as Landlord's lender may reasonably require, provided
hereunder no change shall be made to the Rent, the Term of the Lease, Tenant's Proportionate Share,
or the description of the Premises.

Section 25. Miscellaneous.. If any provision of this Lease or portion of such provision or the
 application thereof to any person or circumstance is for any reason held invalid or unenforceable,
 the remainder of the Lease (or the remainder of such provision) and the application thereof to other
 persons or circumstances shall not be affected thereby. The captions are inserted only as a matter
 of convenience and for reference and in no way define, limit or describe the scope of this Lease nor
 the intent of any provisions hereof. Neither Landlord nor Tenant has dealt with any broker or agent
 in connection with the negotiation or execution of this Lease.; except, Dan Cregan. Tenant and
 Landlord shall each indemnify the other against all costs, expenses, attorneys' fees, and other
 l iability for commissions or other compensation claimed by any other broker or agent claiming the
 same by, through, or under the indemnifying party, This Lease is made pursuant to, and shall be
governed by, and construed in accordance with, the laws of the State of Rhode Island. Tenant shall
 not record this Lease, nor any short form memorandum of this Lease, and any such recording shall
constitute an automatic defhult tinder this Lease. Nothing herein contained shall be interpreted as
creating a partnership or joint venture relationship between Landlord and Tenant, or any other legal
 relationship other than as Landlord and Tenant. If Tenant is comprised of more than one person or
entity, then all covenants, obligations, and liabilities hereunder shall be the joint and several
covenants, obligations, and l iabilities of each such person andior entity. This Lease may be
executed in several counterparts and on separate signature pages, each of which shall be deemed an
             but all of which shall constitute one and the same instrument. Delivery °Ian executed
counterpart of this Lease by telecopier shall be effective as delivery of an original executed
counterpart. From time to time, Tenant shall furnish to any party designated by Landlord, within
ten (10) days after Landlord has made a request therefore, a certificate signed by Tenant confirming
a nd containing such factual certifications and representations as to this Lease as Landlord may
reasonably request in writing, The submission of any draft of this Lease to Tenant shall not be
construed as an offer, and Tenant shall not have. any rights under this Lease unless and until it has
signed a copy of this Lease and delivered it to Landlord, and Landlord has signed a copy of this
Lease and delivered it to Tenant. Tenant acknowledges that the terms and conditions of this Lease
are to remain confidential for the Landlord's benefit, and may not be disclosed by Tenant to
anyone, by any mariner or means, directly or indirectly, without Landlord's prior written consent,
not to be unreasonably withheld if such disclosure has a legitimate and reasonably necessary
business purpose (in any event, Landlord may impose reasonable conditions on any such
disclosure, and the consent by the Landlord to any disclosures shall not be deemed to be a waiver
on the part of the Landlord of any prohibition against any future disclosure). Section f: Items 1
through 12 above arc not to be revealed by Tenant to any past, present or future Tenants of68
Cumberland Street, Woonsocket, RI is a violation or this Lease provision shall be considered a

                                       EXHIBIT "1"
                              TENANT RULES AND REGULATIONS

1.      Tenant shall not perform any acts or early on any practice which may harm the common
areas or be a nuisance to any other Tenant in the Building or the Property,

2.       Unless Landlord shall first give its consent in writing, Tenant will not (a) interfere with the
use of the common areas by any other Tenant or by the employees, agents, guests or invitees of any
other Tenant, or (b) allow its employees to park their motor vehicles within any prohibited areas of
the parking deck or lot so designated by the Landlord.

3.      Plumbing facilities shall not be used for any purpose other than those for which they were
constructed. Tenant shall be responsible for the cost to repair any and all damage resulting from the
misuse of such facilities by Tenant or by its employees, agents, guests, or invitees.

4.      Nothing shall be thrown or swept out of doors of the Premises onto sidewalks, entrances, or
passages, courts, plazas, vestibules, stairways, corridors, or halls of the common areas.

5.     No bicycles, vehicles, or animals of any kind shall be brought into or kept in or about the
Premises.

6.       All repairs done by Tenant shall be made only according to plans and specifications and only
by persons approved by Landlord. Before any repairs arc made by Tenant under the Lease and/or
under these Rules and Regulations are commenced, except in the case of emergency repairs, Tenant
shall provide written notice to Landlord specifying the date that such repairs shall commence, the
projected completion date of the repairs and the names of all persons who will be engaged in
performing the repair work. Landlord shall be entitled to inspect the repair work while in progress
and upon completion.

7.       Any and all requests by Tenant for assistance from Landlord's maintenance crew shall he
made, writing, to Landlord or its representative. Landlord shall, at its discretion, determine whether
       in
and at what time such repairs and maintenance shall be performed by the maintenance crew, provided
however, Landlord agrees that it will not act unreasonable or arbitrarily in making such determination
and shall commence all repair and maintenance work for which Landlord is responsible as soon as
reasonably possible. Tenant shall reimburse Landlord, within thirty (30) days after receipt of a
written statement from Landlord, for all costs incurred by Landlord in effecting repairs and
maintenance at the request and on behalf of Tenant that arc not the responsibility of Landlord under
the Lease,

8.      Canvassing, soliciting, and peddling in the Building or around the Property is prohibited and
Tenant shall cooperate to prevent the same.

9.      Tenant, its employees, agents, guests or invitees, shall not cause or permit any wasteful or
excessive use of water, heat, gas or electricity in or about the Building or the Property.

1 0.      Landlord reserves the right to waive any rule or regulation in any particular instance or as
to any particular person or occurrence and further, Landlord reserves the right to amend or rescind
any of these rules and regulations or to make, amend, and rescind new rules and regulations to the
extent Landlord, in its reasonable judgment, deems suitable for the safety, care, cleanliness, and more
efficient maintenance ofthe Building and the Property.

       This Certificate of Amendment to the Certificate of Formation of Scopia Home Holdings,
LLC (the "LLC") is being duly executed and filed by David Wittels, as the sole member, to
amend the Certificate of Formation under the Delaware Limited Liability Company Act(6
Del.C. § 18-101, et lea.), as amended from time to time.

                                      Independent Auditors' Report


    The Board of Directors
    Community Based Care Intermediate I, LP:

    Opinion

    We have audited the accompanying consolidated financial statements of Community Based Care
    Intermediate I, LP and Subsidiaries (the "Company" or the "Successor"), which comprise the
    consolidated balance sheet as of December 31, 2021 (Successor) and the related consolidated
    statements of operations, members' equity and cash flows for the period from September 16,
    2021 through December 31, 2021 (Successor), and the related notes to the consolidated financial
    statements.

    In our opinion, the consolidated financial statements referred to above present fairly, in all
    material respects, the financial position of Community Based Care Intermediate I, LP and
    Subsidiaries as of December 31, 2021 (Successor), and the results of their operations and their
    cash flows for the period from September 16, 2021 through December 31, 2021 (Successor), in
    accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America.

    Basis for Opinion

    We conducted our audits in accordance with auditing standards generally accepted in the United
    States of America. Our responsibilities under those standards are further described in the
    Auditors' Responsibilities for the Audit of the Consolidated Financial Statements section of our
    report. We are required to be independent of Community Based Care Intermediate I, LP and
    Subsidiaries and to meet our other ethical responsibilities in accordance with the relevant ethical
    requirements relating to our audits. We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is
    sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our audit opinion.

    Responsibilities of Management for the Consolidated Financial Statements

    Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of the consolidated financial
    statements in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of
    America, and for the design, implementation, and maintenance of internal control relevant to the
    preparation and fair presentation of consolidated financial statements that are free from material
    misstatement, whether due to fraud or error.

    In preparing the consolidated financial statements, management is required to evaluate whether
    there are conditions or events, considered in the aggregate, that raise substantial doubt about
    Community Based Care Intermediate I, LP and Subsidiaries' ability to continue as a going
    concern within one year after the date that the consolidated financial statements are available to
    be issued.

===== PAGE 184 =====
Auditors' Responsibilities for the Audit of the Consolidated Financial Statements

Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the consolidated financial
statements as a whole are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to
issue an auditors' report that includes our opinion. Reasonable assurance is a high level of
assurance but is not absolute assurance and therefore is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in
accordance with generally accepted auditing standards will always detect a material
misstatement when it exists. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from
fraud is higher than for one resulting from error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery,
intentional omissions, misrepresentations, or the override of internal control. Misstatements,
including omissions, are considered material if there is a substantial likelihood that, individually
or in the aggregate, they would influence the judgment made by a reasonable user based on the
consolidated financial statements.

In performing an audit in accordance with generally accepted auditing standards, we:

   • Exercise professional judgment and maintain professional skepticism throughout the
     audit.

   • Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement of the consolidated financial
     statements, whether due to fraud or error, and design and perform audit procedures
     responsive to those risks. Such procedures include examining, on a test basis, evidence
     regarding the amounts and disclosures in the consolidated financial statements.

   • Obtain an understanding of internal control relevant to the audit in order to design audit
     procedures that are appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the purpose of
     expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of Community Based Care Intermediate I, LP
     and Subsidiaries' internal control. Accordingly, no such opinion is expressed.

   • Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of
     significant accounting estimates made by management, as well as evaluate the overall
     presentation ofthe consolidated financial statements.

   • Conclude whether, in our judgment, there are conditions or events, considered in the
     aggregate, that raise substantial doubt about Community Based Care Intermediate I, LP
     and Subsidiaries' ability to continue as a going concern for a reasonable period of time.

We are required to communicate with those charged with governance regarding, among other
matters, the planned scope and timing of the audit, significant audit findings, and certain internal
control related matters that we identified during the audit.

Operations and Summary of Significant Accounting Policies

(a)      Operations

Community Based Care Intermediate I, LP ("CBC Intermediate" or "Successor") is a
Delaware limited partnership formed on August 2, 2021, for the purpose of holding
investments in subsidiary companies. Community Based Care Acquisition, Inc.("CBC
Acquisition") was incorporated in Delaware on August 2, 2021, for the purpose of
executing the purchase of 100% of the ownership interest of Community Based Care,
LLC and Subsidiaries. Community Based Care, LLC ("CBC" or "Predecessor") is a
Delaware Limited Liability Company established June 30, 2015, for the purpose of
owning and holding the stock of companies that provide support services for individuals
with intellectual and developmental disabilities. CBC provides leadership and support to
its subsidiary holdings in their provision of quality services to individuals with
disabilities and home care services for the aging. CBC Intermediate owns 100% of CBC
Acquisition, CBC Acquisition owns 100% of CBC, and CBC owns 100% of the equity of
the following companies:

      Homecare Management Corporation ("Homecare") effective August 14, 2015
      Hughes Behavioral and Mental Health Services, Inc.("Hughes") effective September
       18, 2015
      Lindley Habilitation Services, Inc.("Lindley") effective December 2, 2016
      Community Innovations, Inc.("CI") effective April 13, 2017
      Suncoast New Options, Inc.("Suncoast") effective June 30, 2018
      Pinnacle Home Care, Inc.("Pinnacle") effective July 31, 2019
      Quality Home Staffing, Inc.("QHS") effective July 31, 2020
      F.A.C.E.S. Community Service LLC("FACES")effective June 1, 2021
      Group Home Holdings, LLC ("GHH") effective July 1, 2021, as a wholly owned
       subsidiary of Suncoast.

Homecare, Hughes, Lindley, Pinnacle and QHS provide community based services and
services for the aging under the North Carolina Innovations Medicaid Waiver program
and services funded by the State of North Carolina. In addition to these services, CI
provides services to individuals with intellectual and developmental disabilities in ICF-
IID and DDA group home settings. Suncoast provides in home and community based
I/DD services under the Florida Medicaid Waiver program and GHH provides services to
individuals with intellectual and developmental disabilities in ICF-IID and DDA group
home settings in Florida. FACES provides home and community based as well as group
home services under the Virginia Developmental Disability Waiver.

 CBC Intermediate and its subsidiaries as referenced above will collectively be referred to
 as "Company". The Company's corporate offices are located in Raleigh, North Carolina.

(a)     Operations, Continued

On September 16, 2021 ("Acquisition date"), CBC Acquisition purchased a 100% stake
in CBC and its subsidiaries (see note 2).

(b)     Basis of Presentation and Principles of Consolidation

 The consolidated financial statements of "Successor" and "Predecessor" have been
 prepared in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States
 of America. In connection with the change of control effected by CBC Acquisition's
 purchase of 100% of the equity of CBC, the Company elected to apply "pushdown"
 accounting by applying the guidance in Accounting Standards Codification Topic
("ASC") 805, Business Combinations, including the recognition of the Company's assets
 and liabilities at fair value as of September 15, 2021, and similarly recognizing goodwill
 calculated based on the terms of the transaction and the fair value of the new basis of net
 assets of the Company. Accordingly, the consolidated financial statements of the
 Company for periods before and after September 15, 2021, reflect different bases of
 accounting, and the financial positions and results of operations of those periods are not
 comparable. Throughout the Company's consolidated financial statements and the
 accompanying notes herein, periods prior to the change of control are identified as
"Predecessor" and periods after the change of control are identified as "Successor." A
 black line separates the Predecessor and Successor financial statements to highlight the
 lack ofcomparability between the two periods.

 The consolidated financial statements include all of the accounts of CBC Intermediate,
 CBC Acquisition, CBC, Homecare, Hughes, Lindley, CI, Suncoast, Pinnacle, QHS,
 FACES, and GHH. All material intercompany accounts and transactions have been
 eliminated.

(c)     Cash and Restricted Cash

 For purposes of reporting cash flows, cash and restricted cash include cash on hand, cash
 in banks, consumers' personal funds, and highly liquid investments with an original
 maturity date ofthree months or less.

 The Company maintains its cash in bank deposit accounts which, at times, may exceed
 federally insured limits. The Company has not experienced any losses in such accounts.
 The Company believes it is not exposed to any significant credit risk on cash.
